<teammate-message teammate_id="vault-architect" color="blue" summary="Vault architecture assessment - entire.io integration recommendation">
## Vault Architecture Assessment: entire.io Integration

### Architecture Fit Analysis

Your vault has a **clear categorical structure** with templated schemas (decisions, experiments, patterns, daily, projects). Entire.io agent context is **event-driven, timestamp-rich metadata** about agent execution - fundamentally different from these categories.

### Integration Options: Ranked Recommendation

**RECOMMENDATION: Option 1 (EntireOps service) - with daily summary aggregation**

| Option | Fit | Implementation Cost | Operational Risk | Tradeoffs |
|--------|-----|-------------------|------------------|-----------|
| **1. EntireOps service** | 95% | ~4h (copy cloud-vault-mcp pattern) | Low (async, isolated) | **BEST: Centralizes control, reuses existing architecture** |
| 2. Event daemon | 70% | ~6h (new daemon pattern) | Medium (another service to manage) | Duplicates sheets pipeline; adds operational burden |
| 3. Manual CLI | 40% | ~1h | Low | Misses real-time context capture; reactive, not proactive |

**Verdict**: Option 1 wins because your vault already has **VaultOps + CompoundOps + ObsidianOps** as service-oriented tools. Entire.io is just another operational data source, not special enough to deserve a separate daemon.

---

### Proposed Integration: Agent Context as Daily Summaries

**Directory structure**:
- Keep `daily/` for human-authored notes
- Create `daily/agent-logs/` for entire.io summaries (auto-populated)

**Frontmatter schema for agent context notes**:
```yaml
---
date: "2026-02-11"
title: "Agent Execution Summary - 2026-02-11T14:30Z"
tags: [agent, execution, entire.io]
status: archived
source: entire.io
session_id: "sess-12345"
agent_names: [researcher, implementer, tester]
---

## Execution Summary
- Total turns: 47
- Functions called: 312
- Errors: 2 (recoverable)

## Key Decisions
[[decision]] - Made during this session

## Context Shifts
- Task: Feature A → Task: Feature B
- Model: Haiku → Sonnet

## Extracted Learnings
- [[pattern-name]] - Identified new pattern
```

**Why this structure**:
- Respects existing `daily/` category (agent summaries ARE daily execution logs)
- Minimal frontmatter (only 3 required fields: date, tags, source)
- Tagging (`agent`, `execution`) allows filtering/querying
- Wiki-links let you cross-reference to decisions/patterns created during that session
- Status `archived` prevents cluttering active work views

---

### Frontmatter Schema Questions

1. **Which option fits best?** → **Option 1 (EntireOps)** - extends existing Cloud Vault MCP as new service alongside VaultOps/CompoundOps
2. **How to structure agent context notes?** → **Daily summaries in `daily/agent-logs/`** - reuses existing daily convention, minimal friction
3. **Frontmatter schema?** → See above (date, title, tags: [agent, execution, entire.io], status: archived, source, session_id)
4. **New `agents/` directory?** → **NO** - Use `daily/agent-logs/` instead. Agent summaries are fundamentally execution logs, not a primary knowledge category
5. **Risks and tradeoffs?**
   - **Risk: Data explosion** → Mitigate: Archive old summaries after 30 days, only ingest "interesting" sessions (errors, decisions made)
   - **Risk: Vault bloat** → Mitigate: Treat summaries as ephemeral; extract real learnings into `patterns/` or `lessons/` manually
   - **Tradeoff: Automation vs. quality** → Daily summaries are high-velocity, low-signal initially. Value comes from post-hoc review during retrospectives

---

### Implementation Roadmap

**Phase 1** (2h): Build EntireOps service
- Copy `VaultOps` pattern from cloud-vault-mcp
- Add `sync_entire_context(session_id, format="daily")` tool
- Test with one session

**Phase 2** (1h): Create `daily/agent-logs/` schema + summarization
- Use Haiku to summarize entire.io JSON into markdown
- Batch writes to reduce vault churn

**Phase 3** (optional): Post-hoc linking
- Retrospective: Extract decisions/patterns from summaries
- Link back via `[[note]]` references

---

**Bottom line**: Treat entire.io as operational telemetry (like logs), not knowledge. The vault's categorical structure (decisions, patterns, concepts) should remain human-curated. Agent context is the **exhaust** that fuels human retrospectives, not a first-class knowledge category.


</teammate-message>

<teammate-message teammate_id="data-graph-specialist" color="yellow" summary="SurrealDB Agent Context Schema Design - 5 node types + 8 relationship types">
## SurrealDB Agent Context Schema Design

I've analyzed the existing 12D graph structure (84 papers + 21 concepts + 148 relationships) and designed a comprehensive agent context integration. Here's the schema:

---

### 1. NEW NODE TYPES (5 Core Entities)

**agent_session**
- `agent_id` (string) - Unique agent identifier
- `session_id` (string) - Timestamp-based session UUID
- `start_time` (datetime)
- `end_time` (datetime, nullable)
- `model_used` (string) - e.g., "claude-haiku-4-5", "gpt-4"
- `total_tokens` (int)
- `cost_usd` (float)
- `phase` (string) - e.g., "research", "decision", "implementation"
- `status` (string) - "in_progress", "completed", "failed"
- `goals` (array<string>) - Initial goals for session
- `outcome_summary` (string, nullable) - Final summary if completed
- **Rationale**: Captures agent work boundary + resource metrics for efficiency tracking

**agent_decision**
- `decision_id` (string)
- `session_id` (relation) → agent_session
- `decision_type` (string) - "architecture", "feature", "refactor", "bugfix", "data"
- `timestamp` (datetime)
- `reasoning` (string) - Why this decision was made
- `confidence_score` (float 0-1) - How confident was agent in decision
- `validation_status` (string) - "pending", "validated", "invalidated"
- `implementation_status` (string) - "proposed", "in_progress", "completed", "abandoned"
- `metadata` (object) - Tool-specific context (e.g., files modified, tests added)
- **Rationale**: Traces specific architectural/feature decisions to their reasoning + outcome

**agent_reasoning**
- `reasoning_id` (string)
- `decision_id` (relation) → agent_decision
- `reasoning_type` (string) - "research_based", "pattern_based", "intuition", "convention"
- `source_notes` (array<string>) - References to papers/concepts that influenced reasoning
- `chain_of_thought` (string) - Step-by-step reasoning breakdown
- `assumptions` (array<string>) - Stated assumptions
- `timestamp` (datetime)
- **Rationale**: Breaks down WHY a decision was made (e.g., "Haiku is faster" = research_based citing paper_X)

**agent_context**
- `context_id` (string)
- `session_id` (relation) → agent_session
- `active_concepts` (array<string>) - Concept IDs in focus
- `active_domains` (array<string>) - Research domains being explored
- `current_goals` (array<string>) - Active goals at this timestamp
- `token_budget_remaining` (int)
- `decision_history` (array<string>) - IDs of prior decisions in session
- `timestamp` (datetime)
- `relevance_snapshot` (object) - 12D dimension values for papers at this moment
- **Rationale**: Tracks evolving agent state for "what was agent thinking at time T"

**agent_outcome**
- `outcome_id` (string)
- `session_id` (relation) → agent_session
- `outcome_type` (string) - "success", "partial", "failed"
- `lessons_learned` (array<string>) - Lessons extracted (refs to lessons/ notes)
- `metrics` (object) - session_duration_min, token_efficiency_ratio, features_delivered, bugs_fixed
- `validated_by` (string, nullable) - Who validated the outcome
- `timestamp` (datetime)
- `next_recommendations` (array<string>) - What to do next based on outcome
- **Rationale**: Closure node - captures learnings + validation for feedback loop

---

### 2. RELATIONSHIP TYPES (8 Core Edges)

| Edge Type | From | To | Properties | Query Use Case |
|-----------|------|-----|-----------|-----------------|
| **APPLIED_RESEARCH** | agent_decision | paper | `relevance_score` (0-1), `applied_at` (datetime) | "Which papers influenced this agent's decisions?" |
| **VALIDATES_LESSON** | agent_outcome | lesson | `alignment_score` (0-1), `validation_type` ("confirms", "refutes", "refines") | "What lessons come from this agent's work?" |
| **CHALLENGES_LESSON** | agent_reasoning | lesson | `challenge_type` ("contradicts", "limits", "extends") | "Where is agent reasoning misaligned with research?" |
| **INFLUENCED_BY_CONCEPT** | agent_decision | concept | `influence_strength` (0-1), `application_context` (string) | "Did agent apply cross-cutting concepts?" |
| **IMPLEMENTS_PATTERN** | agent_decision | pattern | `pattern_match_score` (0-1) | "Did agent reuse established patterns?" |
| **RELATES_TO_DECISION** | agent_decision | agent_decision | `dependency_type` ("blocks", "enables", "refines") | "How did prior decisions cascade?" |
| **EXPLORES_DOMAIN** | agent_context | domain | `exploration_depth` (0-1), `duration_min` (int) | "Which domains did agent focus on?" |
| **INFORMS_AGENT** | reasoning | agent_context | `information_value` (0-1), `timestamp` (datetime) | "What shaped agent's current context?" |

---

### 3. SCHEMA QUERIES (Strategic Questions)

**Query 1: Papers Influencing Agent Decisions**
```sql
-- "Which papers influenced this agent's decisions?"
SELECT 
  agent_decision.*,
  ->APPLIED_RESEARCH->paper.title,
  ->APPLIED_RESEARCH.relevance_score
FROM agent_decision
WHERE session_id = $session_id
  AND ->APPLIED_RESEARCH EXISTS
ORDER BY ->APPLIED_RESEARCH.relevance_score DESC;
```

**Query 2: Lessons Validated by Agent Work**
```sql
-- "What lessons come from this agent's work?"
SELECT 
  agent_outcome.lessons_learned,
  ->VALIDATES_LESSON->lesson.{title, severity},
  ->VALIDATES_LESSON.alignment_score
FROM agent_outcome
WHERE session_id = $session_id
ORDER BY ->VALIDATES_LESSON.alignment_score DESC;
```

**Query 3: Misalignment Detection**
```sql
-- "Where is agent reasoning misaligned with research?"
SELECT 
  agent_reasoning.*,
  ->CHALLENGES_LESSON->lesson.{title, primary_source},
  agent_reasoning.source_notes
FROM agent_reasoning
WHERE decision_id->session_id = $session_id
  AND ->CHALLENGES_LESSON.challenge_type = "contradicts"
ORDER BY ->CHALLENGES_LESSON.challenge_type;
```

**Query 4: Decision Cascade Analysis**
```sql
-- "How did prior decisions cascade through the session?"
SELECT 
  agent_decision.*,
  <-RELATES_TO_DECISION<-agent_decision.{id, decision_type, timestamp},
  ->RELATES_TO_DECISION->agent_decision.{id, decision_type, timestamp}
FROM agent_decision
WHERE session_id = $session_id
ORDER BY timestamp ASC;
```

**Query 5: Context → Decision Traceability**
```sql
-- "What context conditions led to this decision?"
SELECT 
  agent_decision.*,
  <-RELATES_TO_DECISION<-agent_context.{
    active_concepts,
    active_domains,
    token_budget_remaining,
    timestamp
  }
FROM agent_decision
WHERE id = $decision_id;
```

---

### 4. FIELD DEFINITIONS & METRICS

**Core Metrics to Track**:
- `token_efficiency_ratio` = (output_value / total_tokens) - For Haiku vs Sonnet comparisons
- `decision_validation_rate` = (validated_decisions / total_decisions) - Quality signal
- `research_alignment_score` = (avg APPLIED_RESEARCH.relevance_score) - How much agent uses research
- `lesson_integration_score` = (lessons_validated / available_lessons) - Learning capture
- `domain_exploration_entropy` = Shannon entropy of domain distribution - Breadth vs depth

**Dimensional Mapping** (from 12D graph):
```python
agent_context.relevance_snapshot = {
  "dim_temporal": agent_session.start_time,
  "dim_domain": agent_context.active_domains,
  "dim_connectivity": count(agent_decision->APPLIED_RESEARCH),
  "dim_agent_affinity": 1.0,  # Always 1.0 for agent's own context
  "dim_depth": avg(agent_reasoning.source_notes count),
  "dim_citations": count(->VALIDATES_LESSON),
  # ... etc for all 12 dimensions
}
```

---

### 5. INTEGRATION POINTS

**With Cloud Vault MCP**:
- New tool: `track_agent_context(session_id, goals, active_concepts)` → creates agent_context node
- New tool: `record_decision(session_id, type, reasoning, confidence)` → creates agent_decision + agent_reasoning
- New tool: `validate_outcome(session_id, lessons, metrics)` → creates agent_outcome node

**With Papers/Concepts/Lessons**:
- Bidirectional links (agent can reference papers; papers can reference agent sessions)
- Cross-reference in Obsidian frontmatter: `agent_sessions: [s001, s002]`

**With 12D Graph**:
- agent_context.relevance_snapshot feeds Dimension 12 (Agent Affinity)
- Live queries update paper relevance scores as agent context changes

---

### 6. WHICH NODE TYPES ARE MOST VALUABLE?

**Ranking by ROI**:

1. **agent_decision** (HIGH) - Links decisions to research/patterns/lessons automatically. Captures "why"
2. **agent_outcome** (HIGH) - Validates what agent learned. Closes feedback loop for improvement
3. **agent_reasoning** (MEDIUM) - Detects misalignment with research. Requires explicit chain-of-thought capture
4. **agent_context** (MEDIUM) - Enables "what was agent thinking" queries. Useful for retrospectives
5. **agent_session** (FOUNDATIONAL) - Required wrapper. Low info density but necessary

**Phased Rollout Recommendation**:
- **Phase 1**: agent_session + agent_decision + APPLIED_RESEARCH + VALIDATES_LESSON (2-3 days)
- **Phase 2**: agent_reasoning + CHALLENGES_LESSON + decision cascade tracking (1 day)
- **Phase 3**: agent_context snapshots + relevance_snapshot integration (1 day)
- **Phase 4**: agent_outcome + advanced metrics + agent_affinity dimension (2 days)

---

### 7. IMPLEMENTATION PATTERN

**MCP Tool Example**:
```python
@mcp.tool()
def record_agent_decision(
    session_id: str,
    decision_type: str,  # "architecture", "feature", etc.
    reasoning: str,      # Chain of thought
    papers_applied: list[str],  # Paper IDs that informed this
    confidence_score: float
) -> str:
    """Record architectural decision with research lineage.
    
    Creates:
    - agent_decision node
    - agent_reasoning node
    - APPLIED_RESEARCH edges to cited papers
    
    Returns: decision_id for later validation
    """
    db = Surreal()
    decision_id = f"decision:{uuid.uuid4()}"
    
    # Create decision node
    db.create(f"agent_decision:{decision_id}", {
        "session_id": session_id,
        "decision_type": decision_type,
        "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
        "reasoning": reasoning,
        "confidence_score": confidence_score,
        "validation_status": "pending"
    })
    
    # Create reasoning node
    db.create(f"agent_reasoning:{uuid.uuid4()}", {
        "decision_id": decision_id,
        "reasoning_type": "research_based",
        "chain_of_thought": reasoning,
        "source_notes": papers_applied
    })
    
    # Link to papers
    for paper_id in papers_applied:
        db.relate(f"agent_decision:{decision_id}", "APPLIED_RESEARCH", 
                 f"paper:{paper_id}", {
                     "relevance_score": 0.8,  # Can be refined by LLM
                     "applied_at": datetime.now().isoformat()
                 })
    
    return decision_id
```

---

### Key Insights

1. **Research Lineage**: Every decision can be traced back to papers that influenced it
2. **Lesson Loop**: Agent outcomes validate (or challenge) existing lessons in the vault
3. **Misalignment Detection**: Reasoning nodes can flag where agent contradicts research
4. **Decision Cascades**: Relationships capture how one decision enables/blocks/refines others
5. **Agent Affinity**: Feeds directly into Dimension 12 of the 12D graph for visualization

This schema enables both **operational tracking** (token efficiency, decision quality) and **strategic learning** (research alignment, lesson validation).

---

**Next Step**: Should I implement Phase 1 (agent_session + agent_decision + APPLIED_RESEARCH)? That's the MVP that unlocks research lineage tracking.
</teammate-message>

<teammate-message teammate_id="observability-specialist" color="green" summary="Strategic observability guide: 5 priorities, structures, and 4 anti-patterns">
# Strategic Guidance: Observability & Agent Context Capture

## Executive Summary

Entire.io + your vault creates a **theory-practice feedback loop**. Current gap: You have 39 lessons (operational) but no systematic way to trace *which decisions/actions generated them*. Capturing the right signals closes this loop and accelerates learning.

**Recommended approach**: Capture 20% of signals that generate 80% of learning value, with vault as the system-of-record.

---

## 1. WHAT TO CAPTURE (Priority Order)

### TIER 1 - CAPTURE THESE (High Learning Value)

**1a. Decision Reasoning Chains** ⭐⭐⭐
- *What*: Why agent chose this tool/approach over alternatives
- *Why valuable*: Directly feeds compound engineering + pattern extraction
- *How to structure*: Add `decision_context` section to decision files
  ```yaml
  decision_reasoning:
    - option: Use Ollama embeddings vs API
    - chosen_rationale: "Cost $0 vs $15, local control, latency <100ms"
    - alternatives_rejected: ["OpenAI (cost)", "Anthropic (dependency)"]
    - confidence: 0.95
    - agent_reasoning_chain: [...]  # entire.io trace
  ```
- *Example use case*: Your "adversarial review" decision (2026-02-10) would capture why 4 agents said "reject compound plan" — feeds future risk assessment

**1b. Error Patterns + Resolution** ⭐⭐⭐
- *What*: What went wrong, why, how fixed, what changed
- *Why valuable*: Fastest path to operational lessons learned
- *How to structure*: Structured error notes in lessons/
  ```yaml
  error_pattern:
    class: "git-repo-bloat"
    root_cause: "Training data committed to .git (418 files, 78K lines)"
    detection_method: "Size spike + file analysis"
    cost: "12 GB wasted, broke CI/CD"
    resolution: "BFG, manual gc, enforcement via .gitignore"
    prevention: "Data governance pattern #3"
  ```
- *Current example*: Your "2026-02-11-discovered-redundant-pack-files" decision captures this well

**1c. Cost/Token Metrics (Contextual)** ⭐⭐
- *What*: Actual spend + token efficiency for each decision type
- *Why valuable*: Trains agents on resource constraints, enables optimization
- *How to structure*: Add to each decision/experiment:
  ```yaml
  metrics:
    actual_cost: $1.65
    estimated_cost: $2.00
    cost_delta_pct: -18%
    token_efficiency: "8K tokens per decision"
    cost_per_lesson_generated: $0.42
  ```
- *Current gap*: Your Kyutai project has perfect metrics; apply this retroactively to 39 existing lessons

**1d. Tool Call Sequences (Pattern Level)** ⭐
- *What*: Which tools in what order generated value (not exhaustive logs)
- *Why valuable*: Informs agent orchestration, optimizes tool invocation chains
- *How to structure*: Summarized in decision/experiment as:
  ```yaml
  execution_pattern:
    sequence: ["Bash:git-status", "Read:files", "Grep:search", "Edit:apply"]
    success_rate: 0.95
    avg_latency_ms: 2400
    blockers: ["Bash-on-agent-blocks-Sheet-ops"]
    optimization: "Move sheet updates to lead thread"
  ```
- *Current example*: Your sheets research pipeline (Phase 1-2 complete) captures this implicitly

---

### TIER 2 - CAPTURE SELECTIVELY (Medium Learning Value)

**2a. Model/Temperature Choices**
- *When to capture*: Only when non-default or when choice was deliberate
- *Example*: "Used Haiku (1/3 cost) vs Sonnet for web research, max_turns=5"
- *Where*: Tag in decision metadata, not full trace
- *Why selective*: Redundant if already logging in entire.io

**2b. Confidence Scores**
- *When to capture*: On high-impact decisions only
- *Example*: "Adversarial review confidence: HIGH (4-agent consensus, data-driven)"
- *Where*: Decision frontmatter
- *Why selective*: Most decisions are low-ambiguity; overloading reduces signal

---

### TIER 3 - DO NOT CAPTURE (High Cost, Low Learning Value)

**❌ Tool Call Logs**
- entire.io already has these
- Storing raw logs in vault = bloat + maintenance burden
- Instead: Summarize patterns in decisions/experiments

**❌ Full Token Usage per Tool**
- Too granular; creates noise
- Capture at decision level only (aggregate)

**❌ Intermediate Reasoning Steps**
- "I read file X, then file Y, then thought about Z"
- Use entire.io trace if needed; vault = conclusions only
- Exception: When step *changed the decision* (document as lesson)

**❌ Negative Results (Exploratory Dead-Ends)**
- Your "2026-02-11-large-repositories-26gb" experiment captures this well
- Pattern: Document if it saves future agents time; discard noise

**❌ Sensitive Context (API Keys, Personal Data, Customer Info)**
- Vault = shared knowledge base
- Sensitive traces stay in entire.io only
- Vault captures sanitized lessons only

---

## 2. DATA STRUCTURE FOR MAXIMUM LEARNING VALUE

### Schema: Decision + Experiment + Lesson Integration

**Current state**: You have separate files for decisions/experiments/lessons
**Optimization**: Link them with a "decision → implementation → lessons learned" chain

```yaml
# decisions/2026-02-11-use-escalation-staged-deployment.md
---
title: "Use Escalation + Staged Deployment for Large Repo Cleanup"
date: "2026-02-11"
status: proposed
tags: [decision, high-impact, cost-saving]

# NEW SECTIONS:
implementation_log: "experiments/2026-02-11-github-repo-cleanup-with-bfg.md"
lessons_generated: 
  - "lessons/lesson-data-discipline-prevent-generated-data-in-git.md"
  - "lessons/lesson-git-aggressive-gc-manual-repack.md"
decision_reasoning:
  chosen: "Escalation (BFG → manual gc → repack) vs all-at-once"
  rationale: "Parallel testing in Phase A + Phase C execution = risk mitigation"
  alternatives: ["Aggressive git gc", "Shallow clone + fresh repo"]
  confidence: 0.92
  
metrics:
  actual_cost: $0
  time_to_decision: 45_min
  lessons_generated: 2
---
```

**Benefits**:
- One lookup traces decision → implementation → lessons
- Enables queries like "What decisions generated the most lessons?"
- Closes feedback loop: decision quality improves based on lesson richness

### Lesson Structure (Add These Fields)

```yaml
# lessons/lesson-data-discipline.md
---
title: "Data Discipline: Prevent Generated Data in Git"
date: "2026-02-11"
severity: CRITICAL
tags: [lesson, data-governance]

# NEW FIELDS:
triggered_by: "decisions/2026-02-11-use-escalation-staged-deployment.md"
decision_errors:
  - "Decision: Commit training data to git (historical)"
    "Impact: 418 files, 78K lines, 12GB bloat"
    "Cost: 3 hours debugging, CI/CD broken"
detection_window: "2026-02-10 to 2026-02-11 (24 hours)"
agent_who_discovered: "integration-engineer + data-graph-specialist"
applicability:
  scope: "All ML/data projects in cohezion"
  exceptions: "Small static datasets <1MB"
  
preventions:
  - "Pre-commit hook: Block .pickle, .csv >1MB, .npy"
  - "Pattern: data-governance-prevention-through-pre-commit-enforcement.md"
  - "Cost of prevention: <5 min setup, $0 ongoing"
---
```

---

## 3. PRIVACY & SECURITY IMPLICATIONS

### Safe to Capture
- ✅ Decision reasoning (no PII)
- ✅ Error patterns (sanitized)
- ✅ Cost metrics (already public in decisions)
- ✅ Tool sequences (no credentials)

### Dangerous — DO NOT CAPTURE
- ❌ API keys, tokens, credentials (even if entire.io has them)
- ❌ PII (names, emails, personal details)
- ❌ Customer/proprietary data
- ❌ System internals that could be exploited (OS paths if your vault is public)

### Mitigation
- Vault treats agent context as **public research knowledge**, not private logs
- Sanitization rule: "If it wouldn't be safe in a GitHub README, don't put it in vault"
- Check before committing: `git diff --cached | grep -E "(api_key|password|secret)"`

---

## 4. FEEDBACK LOOP: LESSONS → FUTURE AGENT DECISIONS

### Current Gap
- Lessons exist (39 notes)
- Agents don't systematically read them before deciding
- Result: "Learn → Forget → Relearn" cycle

### Solution: Integrate Lessons into Agent Context

**Option A (Low Cost, High Value)**: Structured reference in decision templates
```yaml
# Before making any decision, agent reads:
relevant_lessons:
  - "lesson-git-aggressive-gc-manual-repack.md"
  - "lesson-data-discipline-prevent-generated-data-in-git.md"
  - "lesson-adversarial-review-before-execution.md"

decision_guardrails:
  - "Do NOT commit training data (per lesson #12)"
  - "Do NOT skip adversarial review on >2h decisions (per lesson #8)"
  - "Use staged escalation on risky operations (per experiment log #7)"
```

**Option B (Medium Cost, Higher Precision)**: Vector search across lessons
- Embed all 39 lessons using Ollama (cost $0)
- Before agent decides, query: "Similar decisions + lessons from past"
- Return top-3 most relevant lessons
- Your Ollama MCP already has `embed` + `query` tools — wire them in

**Option C (Not Yet Recommended)**: LLM-based lesson synthesis
- "Summarize 39 lessons into 5 agent heuristics"
- Risk: Over-simplification, loses nuance
- Try Option A/B first

### Example: Adversarial Review Decision
**Before (current)**: Agent proposes plan, gets reviewed
**After**: Agent reads lesson "adversarial-review-before-execution", proposes plan WITH pre-emptive risk assessment, gets lighter review

---

## 5. WHAT NOT TO CAPTURE (Anti-Patterns)

### ❌ Anti-Pattern #1: "Exhaustive Decision Logs"
- *What*: Every decision step documented in full
- *Why bad*: 500-line decision files nobody reads
- *Better*: 50-line decisions with links to detailed experiments
- *Your example*: Decision files average ~200 lines (good), some pushing 1K (opportunity)

### ❌ Anti-Pattern #2: "Metrics Without Context"
- *What*: "Cost $0.42, took 47 min, 8192 tokens"
- *Why bad*: Meaningless without baseline (cost vs what? latency vs target?)
- *Better*: "Cost $0.42 vs $2.00 estimate (79% savings), 47 min vs 120 min target (60% faster)"
- *Your example*: Kyutai metrics are excellent (33% ahead, 18% under); apply standard to all

### ❌ Anti-Pattern #3: "Capturing Without Acting"
- *What*: Entire.io + vault overflowing with context, zero feedback to agents
- *Why bad*: Passive observation kills agency
- *Better*: "Lessons → Agent context → Next decision incorporates learning"
- *Your action*: Wire lessons into agent prompts (Option A/B above)

### ❌ Anti-Pattern #4: "Privacy Creep"
- *What*: Capturing "who decided what, when" for accountability
- *Why bad*: Chills agent decision-making, creates audit theater
- *Better*: Capture decisions + reasoning, not agent identity (agent names are tags, not identity)
- *Exception*: If you need compliance audit trail (backup to separate secure system)

---

## 6. IMPLEMENTATION ROADMAP (Next 2 Weeks)

### Week 1: Schema + Retrofitting (Low Cost)
1. Update decision/lesson/experiment templates with new YAML fields
2. Retrofit 10 existing decisions/lessons with decision reasoning + metrics
3. Create decision → lesson linking structure (backreferences)
4. **Cost**: ~5 hours, $0

### Week 2: Integration + Feedback Loop (Medium Cost)
1. Wire Ollama embeddings to lesson search (Option B)
2. Add lesson references to agent decision prompts
3. Test: Agent makes decision, reads relevant lessons, adjusts approach
4. **Cost**: ~8 hours, $0 (Ollama free)

### Week 3+: Continuous Operation
- New decisions automatically reference relevant lessons
- Monthly: Review lessons → identify anti-patterns → update guardrails
- **Quarterly**: Analyze cost/token trends across decision types

---

## 7. ANSWERS TO YOUR SPECIFIC QUESTIONS

**Q1: Most valuable agent context?**
- **#1 Decision reasoning** (why over alternatives) — drives compound engineering
- **#2 Error patterns** (what broke, cost, fix) — fastest path to lessons
- **#3 Cost/token at decision level** — trains resource awareness
- NOT: Tool logs (entire.io has them), intermediate steps (noise), negative results (unless instructive)

**Q2: Structure for maximum learning?**
- Link decisions → experiments → lessons (one chain)
- Add decision_reasoning + metrics to every decision
- Use YAML frontmatter for query-ability (enable future automation)

**Q3: Privacy/security?**
- Safe: Reasoning, patterns, costs, tool sequences
- Dangerous: Credentials, PII, system internals
- Vault = public knowledge base (treat accordingly)

**Q4: Feedback to improve future decisions?**
- **Option A (Week 1)**: Structured lesson references in templates
- **Option B (Week 2)**: Vector search across lessons (Ollama embeddings)
- **Option C (Later)**: Synthesis (too risky, loses nuance)

**Q5: What NOT to capture?**
- Tool call logs (entire.io has them)
- Token granularity (decision level only)
- Intermediate reasoning (unless it changed the decision)
- Exploratory dead-ends (unless they save future time)
- Sensitive data (API keys, PII, system internals)

---

## 8. SUCCESS METRICS (8 Weeks)

| Metric | Target | How to Measure |
|--------|--------|---|
| Lessons linked to decisions | 90%+ | Query vault: decision → lessons |
| Agent reads relevant lessons before deciding | 100% | Log in decision template |
| Cost/lesson generated | <$1.00 | Aggregate from metrics |
| Lesson → decision feedback loop | 3+ examples | Show decisions that incorporated lesson |
| Vault query latency (lesson search) | <500ms | Ollama embed + query timing |
| False positive rates (irrelevant lessons) | <10% | Manual sampling of search results |

---

Low risk, high clarity. Retrofit 10 decisions/lessons with proper structure, then measure:
1. How many lessons are currently linked to decisions? (Currently: unknown)
2. What patterns emerge from decision reasoning?
3. What's the cost-per-lesson across decision types?

From that data, decide if Week 2 (automation) is worth the investment.

<teammate-message teammate_id="integration-engineer" color="purple" summary="Entire.io integration: Option 3 (Event daemon) wins—150-200 LOC, 4-6h build time, proven pattern reuse.">
# Entire.io Integration Assessment

## Quick Recommendation
**Option 3 - Event-Driven Daemon** wins decisively.
- **Build time**: 4-6 hours (not 8-10)
- **Implementation**: 150-200 lines of net new code
- **Pattern reuse**: 95% (sheets_research_daemon blueprint + sheets_bridge API)
- **Flexibility**: Highest (extensible to multiple sync targets)
- **Testing/deployment complexity**: Lowest (proven infrastructure)

---

## Detailed Analysis

### Option 1: Cloud Vault MCP Service (EntireOps)
**Implementation path**: Add to main.py, create entire_ops.py service
- **Build time**: 6-8 hours
- **Code**: ~300-400 lines (new service from scratch)
- **Dependency chain**: 
  - Create EntireOps class (100 LOC)
  - Add MCP tool registration (50 LOC)
  - Write tests (150 LOC)
  - Integration with main.py (50 LOC)
- **Flexibility**: Medium (MCP-only, limited to single-call semantics)
- **Pros**: Integrated into existing MCP surface, accessible via tools
- **Cons**: No event loop, requires manual polling/triggering, harder to test

### Option 2: Manual CLI Wrapper
**Implementation path**: Shell script that calls `entire commit --sync-vault`
- **Build time**: 1-2 hours
- **Code**: ~30-50 lines shell/Python
- **Dependency chain**: None (zero infrastructure)
- **Flexibility**: Very low (brittle, hard to integrate with existing pipelines)
- **Pros**: Minimal code
- **Cons**: No fault tolerance, no retry logic, can't batch, no observability

### Option 3: Event-Driven Daemon (RECOMMENDED)
**Implementation path**: `entire_sync_daemon.py` + CLI entry point
- **Build time**: 4-6 hours
- **Code**: 150-200 net new lines
- **Dependency chain**:
  1. Copy sheets_research_daemon.py pattern (start → WorkQueue + async loop)
  2. Replace "fetch from sheets" with "call entire commit"
  3. Replace "batch update sheets" with "batch update vault" + "sync SurrealDB"
  4. Reuse JSON extraction pattern + Haiku agents for enrichment (optional)
- **Flexibility**: Highest (extensible to multiple sync targets, parallelizable)
- **Pros**: 
  - 95% code reuse from existing daemon
  - Fault-tolerant with DLQ + retry
  - Batch operations (vault + SurrealDB in one cycle)
  - Same testing infrastructure (pytest + fixtures)
  - Systemd-ready (already solved)
- **Cons**: None significant

---

## Dependency Chain (What Must Be Done First)

**Option 3 Critical Path**:
1. ✓ Entire.io API is live (manual-commit mode enabled)
2. Reverse-engineer entire.io output format (5 min investigation)
3. Create `entire_ops.py` class with methods:
   - `fetch_latest_commits()` → fetch entire commit output
   - `parse_commit_metadata()` → extract vault action metadata
   - `batch_to_vault_notes()` → convert to vault note format
4. Copy daemon skeleton from sheets_research_daemon.py
5. Test against manual entire output first (no network dependency)
6. Add to main.py service registration
7. Write 3-5 integration tests
8. Systemd unit + runbook

---

## Reusability of Existing Patterns

| Component | Source | Reuse Cost |
|-----------|--------|-----------|
| **Async polling loop** | sheets_research_daemon | Copy-paste (35 LOC) |
| **WorkQueue/DLQ** | sheets_research_daemon | Copy-paste (150 LOC) |
| **Batch vault updates** | sheets_bridge.py | Direct API call (15 LOC) |
| **SurrealDB sync** | surrealdb_sync.py | Direct API call (20 LOC) |
| **Retry + signal handling** | sheets_research_daemon | Copy-paste (40 LOC) |
| **Health monitoring** | health.py | Extend existing (10 LOC) |
| **Tests** | test fixtures | Adapt (50 LOC) |
| **Systemd/CLI** | sheets_research_main.py | Template (30 LOC) |

**Net new code to write**: 50-75 LOC (core transform logic)
**Total LOC**: 150-200 LOC (mostly copy-paste + adaptation)

---

## Deployment & Testing Complexity

**Option 3 is simplest**:
- Unit tests: Reuse WorkQueue + DLQ test fixtures (copy-paste)
- Integration test: Mock entire.io output as JSON file (no network)
- Load test: Feed 100 commits through daemon, validate throughput
- Systemd: Copy sheets_research_main.py template (20-line edits)
- Observability: Extend health.py with entire_sync metrics (10 LOC)

---

## Recommended Implementation Plan (4-6h)

### Phase 1: Investigation & Setup (30 min)
1. Run `entire commit --since-last` manually, capture output format
2. Determine metadata we need (title, description, affected notes, links)
3. Sketch data model for EntireOps class

### Phase 2: Core Implementation (2-3h)
1. Create `src/mcp_server/entire_ops.py` (100-120 LOC)
   - `EntireOps` class with fetch/parse/transform methods
   - Data classes for CommitMetadata, VaultAction
2. Create `src/mcp_server/entire_sync_daemon.py` (80 LOC, minimal—mostly copy)
   - Copy sheets_research_daemon skeleton
   - Replace "SheetsBridge" with "EntireOps"
   - Reuse WorkQueue, DLQ, async loop
3. Update `config.py` with ENTIRE_ env vars (entire.io path, poll interval)
4. Register in main.py service list (5 LOC)

### Phase 3: Testing & Integration (1-2h)
1. Write 3 unit tests (entire_ops transformations)
2. Write 1 integration test (daemon polling cycle with mock commits)
3. Create systemd service unit (copy from sheets_research_main.py)
4. Test graceful shutdown + DLQ recovery

### Phase 4: Observability (30 min)
1. Extend health.py with `entire_sync_status()`
2. Add daemon status CLI command

---

## Why NOT the Other Options

**Option 1 (MCP service)**: 
- Requires manual triggering or external polling (we'd build Option 3 anyway)
- Harder to test (no event loop, mocking MCP layer)
- Less flexible (single-call semantics, can't batch across commits)

**Option 2 (CLI wrapper)**:
- No fault tolerance or observability
- Can't integrate with vault batch operations
- Will need to rebuild when requirements change

Option 3 unblocks:
- **Phase 2**: Add Haiku enrichment agents for entire commits (reuse agent_coordinator)
- **Phase 3**: Multi-target sync (vault + Confluence + Linear)
- **Phase 4**: Bidirectional sync (vault → entire.io pull requests)
- **Graph sync**: Real-time SurrealDB updates (embed entire commits, link to concepts)

## Agent Context Schema Design - COMPLETE

All design documentation created and committed to vault. Ready for Phase 1 implementation approval.

### Deliverables (3 Documents)

1. **Decision Record** (decisions/2026-02-11-surrealdb-agent-context-schema-design.md)
   - Full ADR format with Context/Decision/Consequences/Alternatives
   - 4-phase rollout plan with timeline
   - Success criteria + risks + mitigation
   - Approval questions for next steps

2. **Detailed Pattern** (patterns/surrealdb-agent-context-schema.md)
   - Complete field specifications for all 5 node types
   - 8 relationship types with properties + use cases
   - 5 query examples answering strategic questions
   - Metrics definitions + 12D graph integration

3. **Visual Reference Guide** (patterns/surrealdb-agent-context-visual-guide.md)
   - Entity-relationship diagram
   - 7 query pattern library with examples
   - Performance tips + indexing strategy
   - 3 common scenarios (review, retrospective, alignment check)

### Schema at a Glance

**5 Node Types**:
- `agent_session` — Work boundary + resource tracking (cost, tokens, model)
- `agent_decision` — Architectural/feature decisions with reasoning + confidence
- `agent_reasoning` — HOW decision was made (research vs intuition vs pattern)
- `agent_context` — Agent state snapshot (active concepts, domains, goals)
- `agent_outcome` — Closure node (lessons learned, validation, metrics)

**8 Edge Types**:
- `APPLIED_RESEARCH` → papers that influenced decision
- `VALIDATES_LESSON` → vault lessons confirmed/refined by agent work
- `CHALLENGES_LESSON` → reasoning that contradicts research (misalignment detection)
- `INFLUENCED_BY_CONCEPT`, `IMPLEMENTS_PATTERN`, `RELATES_TO_DECISION`, `EXPLORES_DOMAIN`, `INFORMS_AGENT`

**Core Capabilities**:
1. Research Lineage: Trace every decision back to papers
2. Lesson Validation: Agent work validates/challenges vault knowledge
3. Misalignment Detection: Flag reasoning contradicting research
4. Decision Cascades: Understand how decisions block/enable/refine
5. 12D Integration: Agent context feeds Dimension 12 (Agent Affinity)

### Phased Rollout (Recommended)

| Phase | Timeline | Nodes/Edges | Enables |
|-------|----------|------------|---------|
| **1** | 2-3 days | session + decision + APPLIED_RESEARCH + VALIDATES_LESSON | Research lineage |
| **2** | 1 day | reasoning + CHALLENGES_LESSON + decision cascades | Misalignment detection |
| **3** | 1 day | context snapshots + relevance_snapshot | Retrospective analysis |
| **4** | 2 days | outcome + metrics + 12D integration | Full strategic learning |

**MVP (Phase 1)**: Answers "Which papers influenced this decision?"
**Full Stack (Phases 1-4)**: Complete research-grounded decision framework

### Answers to Your 5 Questions

**Q1: New node types?**
- `agent_decision` (highest value) — captures "why"
- `agent_outcome` (highest value) — completes feedback loop
- `agent_reasoning` (medium) — detects misalignment
- `agent_context` (medium) — enables retrospectives
- `agent_session` (foundational) — required wrapper

**Q2: Relationships between agent context → papers/lessons/decisions?**
- Agent context → papers: Via APPLIED_RESEARCH (decision edges)
- Agent context → lessons: Via VALIDATES_LESSON (outcome edges)
- Agent context → decisions: Via INFORMS_AGENT + RELATES_TO_DECISION

**Q3: Metrics/dimensions to track?**
- token_efficiency_ratio (Haiku vs Sonnet comparison)
- decision_validation_rate (quality signal)
- research_alignment_score (how much agent uses research)
- lesson_integration_score (learning capture)
- domain_exploration_entropy (breadth vs depth)

**Q4: Schema fields?**
- See detailed pattern document (Section 1-2)
- Example agent_decision: decision_id, session_id, type, timestamp, reasoning, confidence_score, validation_status, metadata

**Q5: Strategic queries?**
- "Which papers influenced this decision?" → APPLIED_RESEARCH ranking
- "What lessons from agent's work?" → VALIDATES_LESSON with alignment scores
- "Where is agent misaligned?" → CHALLENGES_LESSON contradictions
- "How did decisions cascade?" → RELATES_TO_DECISION graph traversal

1. **Review** the 3 design documents (vault committed, ~1121 lines total)
2. **Decide**: Is Phase 1 (research lineage only) the right MVP?
3. **Confirm**: Who should validate agent outcomes (human vs automated)?

### 1. `track_session(agent_id, session_start, context_snapshot)`

- Creates decision node linked to session
- Connects to research papers (cites edges)
- Stores decision_reasoning + evidence metrics
- Returns decision_id

### 3. `record_outcome(decision_id, outcome_result, metrics, lessons_learned)`
- Creates outcome node linked to decision
- Stores quantitative metrics (token cost, execution time, quality score)
- Extracts and links to existing lessons (validation check)
- Marks decision as completed

**Implementation Strategy**:
- Tools follow existing pattern: `@mcp.tool()` decorator in server.py
- Direct SurrealDBSync calls (reuse existing connection pool)
- JSON serialization for complex args (reasoning, evidence arrays)
- Error handling: Graceful fallback if SurrealDB unavailable

- Automatically links to research papers via `APPLIED_RESEARCH` edges
- Stores reasoning, confidence scores, decision type
- Handles partial failures (missing papers logged as warnings)
- Returns `decision_id` + count of created links
- Status: ✅ Implemented, tested, registered

#### Tool 3: `record_outcome()`
- Creates `agent_outcome` nodes with outcome metadata
- Automatically links to lessons via `VALIDATES_LESSON` edges
- Updates session status to "completed"
- Validates lesson existence (missing lessons logged as errors)
- Returns `outcome_id` + count of validated lessons
- Status: ✅ Implemented, tested, registered

<teammate-message teammate_id="integration-engineer" color="purple" summary="Week 1 investigation complete: Entire.io API mapped, daemon architecture designed, implementation ready">
# Week 1 Preparation: Entire.io Investigation COMPLETE ✅

**Status**: API fully mapped, architecture designed, implementation blocked on Phase 1 completion

**Deliverables**:

### 1. Entire.io API Investigation Report
**File**: `/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/2026-02-11-entire-io-api-investigation.md`

**Key Findings**:
- Entire.io is enabled in **manual-commit mode** (checkpoint metadata in git commits)
- No direct CLI API for commits (use git log parsing instead)
- Checkpoint data stored as structured git commit body
- Data includes: timestamp, session summary (bullets), metrics, team status, next actions
- Extractable with high-confidence heuristic parsing (regex + section detection)

**Data Example** (from commit 31a250a):
```
Session Summary (2026-02-10):
✅ Completed semantic linking via Claude Sonnet (78%→90% coverage)
✅ SurrealDB sync: 33 new links imported to 12D graph
✅ Adversarial review: 4 agents exposed plan flaws

Vault Metrics:
- Papers: 87% (73/84)
- Decisions: 88% (15/17)
- TOTAL: 90% (130/144)

Team: 14+ agents across 5 concurrent projects
Status: 🟢 ACCELERATING
Next: Phase B optimization + Kyutai Phase 4
```

### 2. Daemon Design Document
**File**: `/home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/patterns/entire-io-sync-daemon-design.md`

**Architecture**:
```
git log polling (5-min intervals)
  ↓ [EntireOps]
  ├─ parse_commit_metadata()
  ├─ extract_metrics()
  └─ extract_outcomes()
  ↓ [Batch Processing]
  ├─ VaultOps: Create checkpoint notes
  ├─ ObsidianOps: Add wiki-links + frontmatter
  ├─ AgentContextOps: track_session() → SurrealDB
  └─ SurrealDBSync: Sync nodes + edges
  ↓ [Fault Handling]
  ├─ WorkQueue (SQLite): Dedup by commit SHA
  ├─ DLQ: Failed commits (unparseable, missing fields)
  └─ Retry: 3 attempts with exponential backoff
```

**Implementation Modules**:
1. **entire_ops.py** (100-120 LOC)
   - `parse_commit_metadata()` - Extract and structure git data
   - `extract_metrics()` - Parse "Metrics:" section into dict
   - `extract_outcomes()` - Parse "Session Summary:" bullets

2. **entire_sync_daemon.py** (200-250 LOC)
   - `EntireSyncDaemon` - Async polling loop (copy sheets_daemon pattern)
   - `WorkQueue` - Track processed commit SHAs
   - `DeadLetterQueue` - Failed commits
   - `sync_commit()` - Orchestrate vault + SurrealDB sync

3. **entire_main.py** (100-150 LOC)
   - CLI: `start`, `status`, `dlq`, `retry`
   - Signal handling + graceful shutdown
   - Config management

### 3. Risk Analysis & Mitigations

| Risk | Mitigation |
|------|-----------|
| Unstructured commit body | Heuristic parsing (regex) + fallback to raw |
| Git author ≠ agent_id | Config file with author→agent mappings |
| Large git histories | Use `git log --since`, index by date |
| Duplicate processing | WorkQueue dedup by SHA (idempotent ops) |
| Performance on 100+ commits | Batch processing, async I/O |

### 4. Phase Integration Points

**Dependency**: Phase 1 Steps 3-4 (query testing + integration tests)
- Step 3 will validate SurrealDB schema ✅
- Step 4 will prove agent_context tools work ✅
- Then ready to integrate entire.io sync immediately

**Unblocking**: Once Phase 1 complete
- Daemon ready for implementation (7h estimate)
- Can parallelize with Phase 1 Steps 5-6 (documentation + sign-off)
- Target deployment: 2026-02-13 (within Phase 1 window)

### 5. Success Criteria (Implementation Phase)

- [ ] Parse 5+ different commit formats without failures
- [ ] Extract metrics with 90%+ accuracy
- [ ] Process 100 commits in <30 seconds
- [ ] DLQ recovery rate >95%
- [ ] Integration test: Full cycle (poll → parse → vault sync → SurrealDB sync)

## Key Design Decisions

**1. Status always "archived"**
- Immutable once written (prevents accidental edits)
- Clear lifecycle (daily review → extract to lessons)

**2. Optional sections except Execution Summary + Metrics**
- Execution Summary: Core metrics always needed
- Key Decisions, Context Shifts, Learnings: Only if present
- Session Artifacts: Only if created in vault
- Related Research: Queried from SurrealDB

**3. Wiki-links with fallback creation**
- Try to link to existing vault note
- If not found, create new note
- Enables knowledge discovery + auto-enrichment

**4. Validation before write**
- All files validated before vault commit
- Invalid files → DLQ for manual review
- No malformed frontmatter possible

## Lessons Learned

🚀 **PHASE 2 ARCHITECTURE - LOCKED AND READY**

## Phase 2 Schema Design Complete

File: `/vault/decisions/2026-02-12-phase-2-schema-design.md`

### Scope (2-3 day sprint)

**New Node Type**:
- `agent_reasoning` (WHY decisions made)
  - reasoning_type: research, pattern, intuition, convention, hybrid
  - chain_of_thought: step-by-step reasoning
  - assumptions: what was believed true
  - alternatives_rejected: why others were dismissed
  - confidence_score: 0.0 - 1.0

**New Edge Types**:
- `CHALLENGES_LESSON` (contradiction detection)
  - challenge_type: contradicts, limits, refines, extends
  - severity: major, minor, clarification
- `RELATES_TO_DECISION` (decision cascades)
  - dependency_type: blocks, enables, refines, contradicts
  - impact_level: critical, significant, minor

### New Query Patterns
1. Root Cause Analysis: "WHY was this decision made?"
2. Contradiction Detection: "Does this contradict a lesson?"
3. Decision Impact Graph: "How did this cascade?"
4. High-Confidence Decisions: "Which decisions had rigorous reasoning?"

### Phase 2 Deliverables (12h total)
- Step 1 (2h): Schema extension + indexes
- Step 2 (3h): MCP tools (record_reasoning, record_challenge, record_cascade)
- Step 3 (3h): Query testing
- Step 4 (2h): Integration testing
- Step 5 (1h): Documentation
- Step 6 (1h): Sign-off

### Success Criteria
- ✅ All 3 new node/edge types creatable via tools
- ✅ 4 new query patterns working
- ✅ Integration tests 100% pass rate
- ✅ Performance < 500ms per query
- ✅ Zero breaking changes to Phase 1

Phase 4 transforms Cohezion from a **knowledge repository** into an **intelligence engine** that reasons over decisions and provides:

1. **GraphRAG Reasoning** - Understand decision chains and implications

3. **Impact Analysis** - Predict what decisions affect what

- Generate reasoning explanations
- **Owner**: TBD (GraphRAG specialist or existing agent)

- Build scoring framework (weighted algorithm, then Bayesian)
- Compute confidence for all 84 papers
- Implement audit trail for confidence changes
- **Owner**: TBD (Scoring/metrics specialist or existing agent)
- **Output**: 400-500 LOC, 30+ tests, scores with audit trail
- **Timeline**: 3-4 days

### **Track C: Impact & Dependency Analyzer**
- Extract decision dependencies from vault
- Compute impact cascades
- Generate critical path analysis
- **Owner**: TBD (Graph analysis or existing data-graph-specialist)
- **Output**: 500-600 LOC, 35+ tests, impact maps
- **Timeline**: 3-4 days

### **Track D: Dashboard & API (Optional)**
- Real-time decision tracking UI
- REST API for decision queries
- Recommendation suggestions
- **Owner**: TBD (UI/API specialist)
- **Status**: Phase 4B (only if 4A completes ahead of schedule)

- Domain expertise (GraphRAG, ML/Bayesian inference)
- Careful algorithm design (confidence must be trustworthy)
- Extensive testing (reasoning quality matters more than quantity)

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

- **Confidence Scoring System**: 5-factor weighted scoring algorithm (Evidence Quality 30%, Precedent Validation 25%, Expert Agreement 25%, Recency 15%, Completeness 5%)
- **Impact & Dependency Analysis**: PERT-style critical path analysis with cascade propagation for decision dependencies
- **Parallel Track Execution**: 3-track concurrent model (proven 40-45% schedule compression in Phases 1-3)
- **Local Infrastructure First**: SurrealDB + Ollama local ($0 cloud cost, <200ms query latency)
- **Test-Driven Development**: Target 90%+ code coverage, 100% test pass rates
- **Async Daily Checkpoints**: 17:00 UTC protocol for team coordination
- **Compound Engineering Principles**: Vault as source of truth with YAML frontmatter metadata
- **Python/TypeScript Stack**: Mix of Python for backend (SurrealDB, GraphRAG, ML) and TypeScript for visualization

## 4. Errors and Fixes

### Error 1: SurrealDB API Connectivity Issues
- **Problem**: Initial attempts to query SurrealDB via HTTP REST API returned "Unsupported media type" errors
- **Root Cause**: Content-Type header handling and SQL query format incompatibility
- **Fix**: Switched from raw curl commands to Python httpx client with proper header setup
- **Resolution**: Successfully deployed all 4 tables using Python HTTP client

### Error 2: Systemd Service Deployment Constraints
- **Problem**: Entire.io sync daemon failed to start when deployed to system-wide `/etc/systemd/system/` due to group determination errors
- **Root Cause**: Sudo permissions not available in environment; security settings too strict for system-wide deployment
- **Fix**: Deployed to user systemd directory (`~/.config/systemd/user/`) with adjusted service file (removed User= directive, updated MemoryLimit to MemoryMax)
- **Resolution**: Service installed successfully in user systemd context; daemon operational via CLI commands

### Error 3: SurrealDB Table Creation Syntax
- **Problem**: Direct SQL CREATE TABLE statements failed with parse errors when sent through surreal CLI
- **Root Cause**: Shell interpolation and multi-statement handling issues
- **Fix**: Executed each CREATE TABLE statement separately via Python client instead of batch
- **Resolution**: All 4 tables created successfully with individual HTTP requests

### No Negative User Feedback
- Throughout the session, the user consistently approved each step and requested progression to the next phase
- No course corrections were needed
- User maintained clear intent through all phases
